# Repair Records

> Append-only. What was DONE about a REJECT, recorded beside the verdict rather than
> replacing it: what the reviewer found stays true, and the disposition becomes visible.
> A repair closing fewer findings than the verdict raised is PARTIAL and names the rest.
> A finding closed by FILING an artefact is recorded as filed, with the id - 'fixed' and
> 'filed as a known issue' are both legitimate, and telling them apart afterwards is not
> optional.

The supersede is dropped entirely: `register\_mutant` records that a later registration does not supersede, though a review round asked for it, because worst-verdict-wins exists precisely because a genuine correction and an author registering their way out of a survivor are byte-identical - and `retract\_mutant` records the same design implemented and reverted. The draft would have reopened it exactly, since it required the supersede to fire on a differing test name. The unit now AUDITS: nothing reports a duplicate key today, which is the whole of the harm that survives worst-verdict-wins, and correcting one stays with `retract`, which costs a reason and leaves the correction on the record. Four criteria, one through the shipped CLI, one on the corpus.
